It follows me home, and it makes even my garden or my watercolors seem really far away.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd say that. I mean, when I actually make myself sit down with my watercolors or go out to the garden, I still like doing it. It's not that the enjoyment is gone... it's more just that I don't have the energy to start most of the time. So, no, I wouldn't say I've stopped enjoying things, I just haven't been doing them as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I think I'm eating about the same as I usually do. My appetite hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been a while now. I'd say... probably about two years and two months. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home, and our whole household routine changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you felt better in between times when you felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any periods where I felt better. It's just been this same kind of low, tired feeling the whole time. There haven't been any breaks from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure when you're doing things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think I still enjoy things. Um... I mean, if I sit down to read a book or watch a movie, I still get into it and like it. I haven't really noticed a problem with that. It's not that I don't feel pleasure anymore... I just have that same tired feeling while I'm doing those things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um... I just keep thinking I should be handling everything better than I am. Like, my lesson plans for school are taking me twice as long to get through lately, or... or I have to say no to mentoring a newer teacher. When things like that happen, I just tell myself I'm letting everyone down, you know? My family, my school. I just feel like I should be doing a better job with it all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I just keep thinking I should be handling everything better than I am. When things like that happen, I just tell myself I'm letting everyone down, you know? My family, my school. I just feel like I should be doing a better job with it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you didn't feel this low, tired feeling, before it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I mean, before this started a couple of years ago, I didn't feel this way. I had normal energy. It's just... it's been going on for so long now that it's kind of hard to remember exactly what that felt like. But I know I wasn't always this tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of feeling normal or significantly better for at least two months since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... it's pretty much just been this same constant, low feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a break from it where I felt normal again for that long.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to push yourself to feel happy or pleased when you're doing things that are normally pleasurable for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I really have to force myself to try and get anything out of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, when I actually make myself sit down with my watercolors or go out to the garden, I still like doing it. It's not that the enjoyment is gone... it's more just that I don't have the energy to start most of the time. It's just been this same kind of low, tired feeling the whole time. I really have to force myself to try and get anything out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to try extra hard to enjoy things that are normally pleasurable for you?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, like I was just saying... I really do. It takes a lot of extra effort to get anything out of my painting or gardening these days. I pretty much just have to force myself to do them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting less than two months where you felt significantly better since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a steady, flat feeling the whole time. I haven't really had any breaks where I felt like myself again, even for a little while.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to try extra hard to feel any pleasure at all, even when doing things that are normally enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I... I feel like we just covered this, but yes. Like I was just saying with my gardening and my watercolors, I really do have to force myself. I don't really have anything else to add to that, it's just exactly like I was just explaining.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your symptoms were slightly better or slightly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's just been pretty steady. I don't really have days where it feels noticeably worse or days where it feels any better... it's just always kind of the same flat feeling every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your symptoms felt slightly better or slightly worse than they do right now?\n\nPatient: I think you actually just asked me that a second ago. But no, my answer is still the same. I haven't really noticed any periods where it gets better or worse. It's just consistently flat, like I was just saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least two years where you felt significantly better since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. Like I said, it's just been a constant thing for the whole two years and two months. There hasn't been any break in it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your symptoms are worse during certain times of the year?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't seem to matter if it's summer break or the middle of the school year. I just keep thinking I should be handling everything better than I am. Like, my lesson plans for school are taking me twice as long to get through lately, or... or I have to say no to mentoring a newer teacher. When things like that happen, I just tell myself I'm letting everyone down, you know? My family, my school. I just feel like I should be doing a better job with it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you didn't feel this low, tired feeling, before it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I mean, before this started a couple of years ago, I didn't feel this way. I had normal energy. It's just... it's been going on for so long now that it's kind of hard to remember exactly what that felt like. But I know I wasn't always this tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of feeling normal or significantly better for at least two months since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... it's pretty much just been this same constant, low feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a break from it where I felt normal again for that long.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to push yourself to feel happy or pleased when you're doing things that are normally pleasurable for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um... most days I can still smile for my students and, you know, get through the lessons. But underneath that, it just feels like there's this constant gray, flat feeling. It just follows me home. Even things I usually enjoy, like working in my garden or doing watercolors... it all just seems kind of far away now. I really have to force myself to try and get anything out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to try extra hard to enjoy things that are normally pleasurable for you?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, like I was just saying... I really do. It takes a lot of extra effort to get anything out of my painting or gardening these days. I pretty much just have to force myself to do them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting less than two months where you felt significantly better since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a steady, flat feeling the whole time. I haven't really had any breaks where I felt like myself again, even for a little while.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to try extra hard to feel any pleasure at all, even when doing things that are normally enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I... I feel like we just covered this, but yes. Like I was just saying with my gardening and my watercolors, I really do have to force myself. I don't really have anything else to add to that, it's just exactly like I was just explaining.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your symptoms were slightly better or slightly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's just been pretty steady. I don't really have days where it feels noticeably worse or days where it feels any better... it's just always kind of the same flat feeling every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your symptoms felt slightly better or slightly worse than they do right now?\n\nPatient: I think you actually just asked me that a second ago. But no, my answer is still the same. I haven't really noticed any periods where it gets better or worse. It's just consistently flat, like I was just saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least two years where you felt significantly better since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. Like I said, it's just been a constant thing for the whole two years and two months. There hasn't been any break in it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your symptoms are worse during certain times of the year?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't seem to matter if it's summer break or the middle of the school year. It's just the same all year round.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for two years or more?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I mentioned a minute ago, it's been about two years and two months. Um, it basically started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole household routine changed. I've just felt this way ever since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
